independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n           * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n           */\n          const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const {\n              T y = log(gsm::asc_normal(x, m_sL, m_z1));\n              y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n              return y;\n          }\n    };\n\n    // DoubleNormal: 3-parameter double normal (dome shaped) selectivity\n    /**\n     * @brief 3-parameter double normal curve\n     * @details Uses gsm::pdubnorm\u003cT> function.\n     *\n     * @param T data vector or dvar vector\n     * @param T2 double or dvariable for left-hand width, size at dome, right-hand width\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T,class T2>\n    class DoubleNormal: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n      private:\n            T2 m_sL;\n            T2 m_s50;\n            T2 m_sR;\n\n      public:\n        /**\n         * Class constructor.\n         *\n         * @param sL - width of lefthand limb  (default=1)\n         * @param s50 - size at dome           (default=1)\n         * @param sR - width of righthand limb (default=1)\n         */\n            DoubleNormal(T2 sL = T2(1), T2 s50 = T2(1), T2 sR = T2(1))\n            : m_sL(sL), m_s50(s50), m_sR(sR) {}\n\n            T2 GetSL()  const { return m_sL; }\n            T2 GetS50() const { return m_s50; }\n            T2 GetSR()  const { return m_sR; }\n\n            void SetSL(T2 &sL)   { this->m_sL = sL; }\n            void SetS50(T2 &s50) { this->m_s50 = s50; }\n            void SetSR(T2 &sR)   { this->m_sR = sR; }\n            void SetParams(T2 &sL, T2 &s50, T2 &sR){\n                this->m_sL = sL;\n                this->m_s50 = s50;\n                this->m_sR = sR;\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return selex with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                return gsm::pdubnorm(x, m_sL, m_s50, m_sR);\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex)\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return log(selex) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                return log(gsm::pdubnorm(x, m_sL, m_s50, m_sR));\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n            {\n                T y = log(gsm::pdubnorm(x, m_sL, m_s50, m_sR));\n                y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n                return y;\n            }\n    };\n\n    // DoubleNormal4a: Alternative 4-parameter double normal (dome shaped) selectivity with plateau\n    /**\n     * @brief Alternative 4-parameter double normal curve with plateau\n     * @details The width of the plateau is a determined by a scaling factor (sclInc) on the distance between \n     * the size at the ascending peak (ascPkZ) and the maximum allowed size (fsZ). \n     *\n     * @param T data vector or dvar vector\n     * @param T2 double or dvariable\n     * @param T3 double (or can be dvariable if T2 is dvariable)\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T,class T2, class T3>\n    class DoubleNormal4a: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n      private:\n        T2 m_ascWdZ;//ascending limb width\n        T2 m_ascPkZ;//size at which ascending limb reaches 1\n        T2 m_dscWdZ;//descending limb width\n        T2 m_sclInc;//scaled increment to size at start of descending limb\n        T3 m_fsZ;   //maximum possible size at which descending limb departs from 1\n\n      public:\n        /**\n         *\n         * @param ascWdZ - ascending limb width\n         * @param ascPkZ - size at which ascending limb reaches 1\n         * @param dscWdZ - descending limb width\n         * @param sclInc - scaled increment to size at start of descending limb\n         * @param fsZ - max size possible for start of descending limb\n         */\n        DoubleNormal4a(T2 ascWdZ = T2(1), T2 ascPkZ = T2(1),\n                       T2 dscWdZ = T2(1), T2 sclInc = T2(0), T3 fsZ = T3(1))\n        : m_ascWdZ(ascWdZ), m_ascPkZ(ascPkZ), m_dscWdZ(dscWdZ), m_sclInc(sclInc), m_fsZ(fsZ) {}\n\n        T2 GetAscWdZ()  const { return m_ascWdZ; }\n        T2 GetAscPkZ()  const { return m_ascPkZ; }\n        T2 GetDscWdZ()  const { return m_dscWdZ; }\n        T2 GetSclInc()  const { return m_sclInc; }\n        T3 GetFsZ()     const { return m_fsZ; }\n\n\n        void SetAscWdZ(T2 &ascWdZ){ this->m_ascWdZ = ascWdZ; }\n        void SetAscPkZ(T2 &ascPkZ){ this->m_ascPkZ = ascPkZ; }\n        void SetDscWdZ(T2 &dscWdZ){ this->m_dscWdZ = dscWdZ; }\n        void SetSclInc(T2 &sclInc){ this->m_sclInc = sclInc; }\n        void SetFsZ(T3 &fsZ){ this->m_fsZ = fsZ; }\n        void SetParams(T2 &ascWdZ, T2 &ascPkZ, T2 &dscWdZ, T2 &sclInc, T3 &fsZ){\n            this->m_ascWdZ = ascWdZ;\n            this->m_ascPkZ = ascPkZ;\n            this->m_dscWdZ = dscWdZ;\n            this->m_sclInc = sclInc;\n            this->m_fsZ = fsZ;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief selex\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return selex with type T\n         */\n        const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n            return gsm::pdubnorm4a(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_sclInc, m_fsZ);\n        }\n        /**\n         * @brief log(selex)\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n            return log(gsm::pdubnorm4a(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_sclInc, m_fsZ));\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n        {\n            T y = log(gsm::pdubnorm4a(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_sclInc, m_fsZ));\n            y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n            return y;\n        }\n    };\n\n    // DoubleNormal4: 4-parameter double normal (dome shaped) selectivity\n    /**\n     * @brief 4-parameter double normal curve with plateau\n     * @details The width of the plateau is a determined by the distance between \n     * the size at the ascending peak (ascPkZ) and the size at the descending peak (dscPkZ). \n     *\n     * @param T data vector or dvar vector\n     * @param T2 double or dvariable\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T,class T2>\n    class DoubleNormal4: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n      private:\n        T2 m_ascWdZ;//ascending limb width\n        T2 m_ascPkZ;//size at which ascending limb reaches 1\n        T2 m_dscWdZ;//descending limb width\n        T2 m_dscPkZ;//size at which descending limb departs from 1\n\n      public:\n        /**\n         *\n         * @param ascWdZ - ascending limb width\n         * @param ascPkZ - size at which ascending limb reaches 1\n         * @param dscWdZ - descending limb width\n         * @param dscPkZ - size at which descending limb departs from 1\n         */\n        DoubleNormal4(T2 ascWdZ = T2(1), T2 ascPkZ = T2(1),\n                      T2 dscWdZ = T2(1), T2 dscPkZ = T2(1))\n        : m_ascWdZ(ascWdZ), m_ascPkZ(ascPkZ), m_dscWdZ(dscWdZ), m_dscPkZ(dscPkZ) {}\n\n        T2 GetAscWdZ()  const { return m_ascWdZ; }\n        T2 GetAscPkZ()  const { return m_ascPkZ; }\n        T2 GetDscWdZ()  const { return m_dscWdZ; }\n        T2 GetDscPkZ()  const { return m_dscPkZ; }\n\n        void SetAscWdZ(T2 &ascWdZ){ this->m_ascWdZ = ascWdZ; }\n        void SetAscPkZ(T2 &ascPkZ){ this->m_ascPkZ = ascPkZ; }\n        void SetDscWdZ(T2 &dscWdZ){ this->m_dscWdZ = dscWdZ; }\n        void SetDscPkZ(T2 &dscPkZ){ this->m_dscPkZ = dscPkZ; }\n        void SetParams(T2 &ascWdZ, T2 &ascPkZ, T2 &dscWdZ, T2 &dscPkZ){\n            this->m_ascWdZ = ascWdZ;\n            this->m_ascPkZ = ascPkZ;\n            this->m_dscWdZ = dscWdZ;\n            this->m_dscPkZ = dscPkZ;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief selex\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return selex with type T\n         */\n        const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n            return gsm::pdubnorm4(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_dscPkZ);\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief calculate log(selex)\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n            return log(gsm::pdubnorm4(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_dscPkZ));\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n        {\n            T y = log(gsm::pdubnorm4(x, m_ascWdZ, m_ascPkZ, m_dscWdZ, m_dscPkZ));\n            y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n            return y;\n        }\n    };\n\n    // StackedLogistic: 5-parameter stacked logistic selectivity\n    /**\n     * @brief 5-parameter stacked logistic selectivity\n     * @details Uses the logistic curve (plogis95) for a two parameter function\n     *\n     * @param T data vector or dvar vector\n     * @param T2 double or dvariable\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T,class T2>\n    class StackedLogistic: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n    private:\n        T2 m_omega;     // weighting factor\n        T2 m_mean1;     // mean for the first logistic curve\n        T2 m_sd1;       // sd for the first logistic curve\n        T2 m_mean2;     // mean for the second logistic curve\n        T2 m_sd2;       // sd for the first logistic curve\n\n    public:\n        /**\n         *\n         * @param omega - weighting factor\n         * @param mean1 - mean for the first logistic curve\n         * @param sd1   - sd for the first logistic curve\n         * @param mean2 - mean for the second logistic curve\n         * @param sd2   - sd for the first logistic curve\n         */\n        StackedLogistic(T2 omega = T2(1), T2 mean1 = T2(1),\n                      T2 sd1 = T2(1), T2 mean2 = T2(1), T2 sd2 = T2(1))\n        : m_omega(omega), m_mean1(mean1), m_sd1(sd1), m_mean2(mean2), m_sd2(sd2) {}\n\n        T2 GetOmega()  const { return m_omega; }\n        T2 GetMean1()  const { return m_mean1; }\n        T2 GetSd1()    const { return m_sd1; }\n        T2 GetMean2()  const { return m_mean2; }\n        T2 GetSd2()    const { return m_sd2; }\n\n        void SetOmega(T2 &omega){ this->m_omega = omega; }\n        void SetMean1(T2 &mean1){ this->m_mean1 = mean1; }\n        void SetSd1(T2 &sd1)    { this->m_sd1 = sd1; }\n        void SetMean2(T2 &mean2){ this->m_mean2 = mean2; }\n        void SetSd2(T2 &sd2)    { this->m_sd2 = sd2; }\n        void SetParams(T2 &omega, T2 &mean1, T2 &sd1, T2 &mean2, T2 &sd2){\n            this->m_omega = omega;\n            this->m_mean1 = mean1;\n            this->m_sd1   = sd1;\n            this->m_mean2 = mean2;\n            this->m_sd2   = sd2;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief selex\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return selex with type T\n         */\n        const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n             T y1 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean1, m_sd1);\n             T y2 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean2, m_sd2);\n             T y = m_omega*y1 + (1-m_omega)*y2;\n            return y;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief calculate log(selex)\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n        {\n             T y1 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean1, m_sd1);\n             T y2 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean2, m_sd2);\n             T y = m_omega*y1 + (1-m_omega)*y2;\n             y = log(y);\n            return y;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n         * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n         * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n         */\n        const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n        {\n             T y1 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean1, m_sd1);\n             T y2 = gsm::plogis(x, m_mean2, m_sd2);\n             T y = m_omega*y1 + (1-m_omega)*y2;\n             y = log(y);\n             y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n            return y;\n        }\n    };\n\n    // Coefficients: Base function for non-parametric selectivity cooefficients\n    /**\n     * @brief Nonparametric selectivity coefficients\n     * @details The length of the vector sel_coeffs must be less than or equal to\n     * length of the independent variabls x.  If it it shorter, then it is assumed that\n     * the parameters in the last element of sel_coeffs is the same for all elements in\n     * the terminus of the independent vector.  Also use a logit transformation to ensure\n     * that selectivities are bounded between 0-1.\n     *\n     * @param x Independent variable\n     * @param sel_coeffs Vector of estimated selectivity coefficients logit (??: exponential?) transformed.\n     * @return Selectivity coefficients.\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T>\n    const T coefficients(const T &x, const T &sel_coeffs)\n    {\n            int x1 = x.indexmin();\n            int x2 = x.indexmax();\n            int y2 = sel_coeffs.indexmax();\n            T y = 0.0*x;\n            for ( int i = x1; i \u003c y2; i++ )\n            {\n                    y(i) = mfexp(sel_coeffs(i));\n            }\n            y(y2,x2) = mfexp(sel_coeffs(y2));\n            return y;\n    }\n\n    // SelectivityCoefficients: Age/size-specific selectivity coefficients for n-1 age/size classes\n    /**\n     * @brief Selectivity coefficients\n     * @details Age or size-specific selectivity coefficients for n-1 age/size classes\n     *\n     * @tparam T vector of coefficients\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T>\n    class SelectivityCoefficients: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n    private:\n            T m_sel_coeffs;\n\n    public:\n            SelectivityCoefficients(T params = T(1))\n            :m_sel_coeffs(params) {}\n\n            T GetSelCoeffs() const { return m_sel_coeffs; }\n            void SetSelCoeffs(T &x) { this->m_sel_coeffs = x; }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return selex with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    // Call the age/size specific function\n                    return gsm::coefficients(x, m_sel_coeffs);\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex)\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return log(selex) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    // Call the age/size specific function\n                    return log(gsm::coefficients(x, m_sel_coeffs));\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T y = log(gsm::coefficients(x, m_sel_coeffs));\n                    y -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n                    return y;\n            }\n    };\n\n\n    // ParameterPerClass: One age/size-specific selectivity parameter for each age/size class\n    /**\n     * @brief Parametric selectivity function\n     * @details One age or size-specific selectivity parameter for each age/size class.\n     *\n     * Note that the same can be accomplished using the SelectivityCoefficients class but Athol wanted to have this function in CSTAR\n     *\n     * @param T vector of parameters (initial values)\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T>\n    class ParameterPerClass: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n    private:\n            T m_selparms;\n\n    public:\n            ParameterPerClass(T selparms = T(1))\n            :m_selparms(selparms) {}\n\n            T GetSelparms() const { return m_selparms; }\n            void SetSelparms(T &selparms) { this->m_selparms = selparms; }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T\n             * @return selex with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    // Call the age/size specific function\n                    return gsm::nonparametric(x, m_selparms);\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex)\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T\n             * @return log(selex) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    // Call the age/size specific function\n                    return log(gsm::nonparametric(x, m_selparms));\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex) [NOT log(selex/mean(selex))??]\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T y = log(gsm::nonparametric(x, m_selparms));\n                    //y  -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n                    return y;\n            }\n    };\n\n    // UniformCurve: Uniform over a length\n    /**\n     * @brief Flat selectivity\n     * @details All values equal 1 (with type T)\n    **/\n    template\u003cclass T>\n    class UniformCurve: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n\n    public:\n            UniformCurve() {}\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T x - independent variable\n             * @return 1 with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T selex = 1.0 + 0.0*x;\n                    return selex;\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex) = 0\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return 0 with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T selex = 0.0*x;\n                    return selex;\n            }\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return 0 with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T selex = 0.0*x;//WTS: was 1.0+0.0*x, but this was not correct\n                    return selex;\n            }\n\n    };\n\n    /**\n     * @brief Uniform curve with selex = 0.\n     */\n    template\u003cclass T>\n    class Uniform0Curve: public Selex\u003cT>\n    {\n\n    public:\n            Uniform0Curve() {}\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T x - independent variable\n             * @return selex with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                T selex = 0.0*x;\n                    return selex;\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex) [= -100]\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return -100 with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T &x) const\n            {\n                T selex = -100.0 + 0.0*x;\n                    return selex;\n            }\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex)) [= 0 here]\n             * @param T x - independent variable--must be compatible with T2\n             * @return 0 with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T &x) const\n            {\n                    T selex = 0.0*x;//WTS: was 1.0+0.0*x, but this was not correct\n                    return selex;\n            }\n\n    };\n\n\n    // Cubic Spline: Age/size-specific for a cubic split\n    /**\n     * @brief Cubic Spline\n     * @details Cubic spline\n     *\n     * @param y_vals T vector of values at knots (selex type will be T)\n     * @param x_vals T1 vector of knots (must be non-dvar_ if T is non-dvar_)\n    **/\n    template\u003ctypename T, typename T1>\n    class SelectivitySpline: public Selex\u003cT> {\n        public:\n            static int debug;//debugging flag\n\n        private:\n            T  m_yvals; //dependent values in spline (estimated parameters, can be dvar_ or non-dvar_)\n            T1 m_xvals; //independent values in spline (knots, must be non-dvar_ if yvals is non-dvar_\n            T  m_y2vals;//2nd derivatives of m_yvals at m_xvals\n\n\n        public:\n            /**\n             * Constructor for cubic spline. Beginning and ending boundary conditions\n             * on first derivatives are set to 0.\n             *\n             * @param T  yvals - values at knots (can be dvar_ or non-dvar_)\n             * @param T1 xvals - knots           (must be non-dvar_ if yvals is non-dvar_)\n             */\n            SelectivitySpline(const T &yvals, const T1 &xvals){\n                initSpline(yvals,xvals);\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * Calculate second derivatives for spline interpolation.\n             *\n             * @param T yvals\n             * @param T1 xvals\n             */\n            void initSpline(const T &yvals, const T1 &xvals) {\n                if ((m_yvals.indexmin()!=yvals.indexmin())||\n                    (m_yvals.indexmax()!=yvals.indexmax())){\n                    if (debug) cout\u003c\u003c\"initSpline:: deallocating arrays\"\u003c\u003cendl;\n                    m_yvals.deallocate();\n                    m_xvals.deallocate();\n                    m_y2vals.deallocate();\n                }\n                m_yvals = (T&)  yvals;//create shallow, un-const copy\n                m_xvals = (T1&) xvals;//create shallow, un-const copy\n\n                //shift arrays to start at 0\n                int lb = m_xvals.indexmin();//lower bound on x and y indices\n                m_xvals.shift(0);\n                m_yvals.shift(0);\n\n                //calculate second derivatives\n                //cout\u003c\u003c\"--computing 2nd derivatives\"\u003c\u003cendl;\n                int ibcbeg = 1;\n                int ibcend = 1;\n                double ybcbeg = 0.0;\n                double ybcend = 0.0;\n                m_y2vals.allocate(m_xvals);\n                m_y2vals = spline_cubic_set(m_yvals, m_xvals, ibcbeg, ybcbeg, ibcend, ybcend);\n\n                //shift arrays back to start at original lower index\n                m_xvals.shift(lb);\n                m_yvals.shift(lb);\n                m_y2vals.shift(lb);\n                //cout\u003c\u003c\"m_xvals = \"\u003c\u003cm_xvals\u003c\u003cendl;\n                //cout\u003c\u003c\"m_yvals= \"\u003c\u003cm_yvals\u003c\u003cendl;\n                //cout\u003c\u003c\"y2vals  = \"\u003c\u003cm_y2vals\u003c\u003cendl;\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief selex\n             * @param T1 x - independent variable--must be compatible with T\n             * @return selex with type T\n             */\n            const T Selectivity(const T1 &x) const {\n                // do interpolation at x values\n                int mmin = x.indexmin();\n                int mmax = x.indexmax();\n                T selex(x.indexmin(),x.indexmax());\n                T tempX(x.indexmin(),x.indexmax());\n                for (int i=mmin;i\u003c=mmax;i++){\n\t\t\t\t   {\n                    tempX(i) = spline_cubic_val(x(i),m_xvals,m_yvals,m_y2vals);\n                    selex(i) = mfexp(tempX(i))/(1.0+exp(tempX(i)));\n\t\t\t\t   }\n                }\n                return selex;\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex)\n             * @param T1 x - independent variable--must be compatible with T\n             * @return log(selex) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelectivity(const T1 &x) const {\n                return log(Selectivity(x));\n            }\n\n            /**\n             * @brief calculate log(selex/mean(selex))\n             * @param T1 x - independent variable--must be compatible with T\n             * @return log(selex/mean(selex)) with type T\n             */\n            const T logSelexMeanOne(const T1 &x) const {\n                T y = log(Selectivity(x));\n                y -= log(mean(mfexp(y)));\n                return y;\n            }\n    };//SelectivitySpline\u003cT,T1>\n    template\u003ctypename T, typename T1>\n    int SelectivitySpline\u003cT,T1>::debug = <div><tool-result call-id="call_8NLZUwnnQsz1mNAUALz9MJq7"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 5.3KB, 102 lines, showing lines 85-102]\n85\t\n86\twhere \n87\t\n88\t$$ F(f,y,s) = f_c(f,y,...[truncated, total 793 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 5.3KB, 102 lines, showing lines 85-102]\n\nwhere \n\n$$ F(f,y,s) = f_c(f,y,s) \\cdot sel(f,y,s) \\cdot \\{ret(f,y,s) + (1-ret(f,y,s)) \\cdot dmr(f,y,s)\\} $$ {#eq-IFM-TFM}\n\n#### Survey \"dynamics\" {#sec-SrvDy}\n\n\"Survey\" fleets provide relative indices of population abundance by population category, and so survey catch abundance, $C_S(f,y,s)$ is simply proportional to population abundance (although the proportionality constant may depend population category):\n\n$$ C_S(f,y,s) = q(f,y,s) \\cdot sel(f,y,s) \\cdot N(y,s) $$ {#eq-SrvAbd}\n\nwhere $q(f,y,s)$ is the \"fully-selected\" catchability (the proportionality constant when $sel(f,y,s)$ is 1) and $sel(f,y,s)$ is survey selectivity.\n\n\n#### Selectivity/retention functions {sec-SelFcns}\n\n\n"},"elapsedMs":17}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="call_OJPmYPNOrF1SI05jaiwmGoQN"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"bash","input":{"command":"grep -R -n -i -E 'selectivity|selectivities|double.?normal|pdubnorm|selcoffs|spline_cubic|...[truncated, total 369 chars]","description":"Search session history and notes with grep"},"description":"Search session history and notes with grep","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"grep -R -n -i -E 'selectivity|selectivities|double.?normal|pdubnorm|selcoffs|spline_cubic|plogis' /Users/williamstockhausen/Work/Programming/rtmbGMACS/.Rhistory /Users/williamstockhausen/Work/Programming/rtmbGMACS/conversations /Users/williamstockhausen/Work/Programming/rtmbGMACS/notes_for_code /Users/williamstockhausen/Work/Programming/rtmbGMACS/examples | head -100","language":"bash"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_OJPmYPNOrF1SI05jaiwmGoQN"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"(no output)"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"(no output)","language":"text"},"elapsedMs":68}</script></tool-result></div>

All references below are to `Cpp/selex.hpp`. Arguments are in constructor order; functions take `x` first.

Let $L(t)=1/(1+e^{-t})$ and $G(x;p,w)=e^{-\frac12((x-p)/w)^2}$. The C++ uses `mfexp` in several places rather than ordinary `exp`.

- **LogisticCurve / `plogis(mean, sd)`**: $L((x-mean)/sd)$ (lines 105–110, 339–356).
- **LogisticCurve95 / `plogis95(s50, s95)`**: $L(\log(19)(x-s50)/(s95-s50))$ (127–132, 398–415). The class comment says “5%,” but the implementation uses **50%**.
- **LogisticCurveOne / `plogisOne(slope)`**: $1/[1+\exp(slope(x_{\rm last}-x)+\log(1/0.999-1))]$ (148–154, 458–473). Table 1 incorrectly calls this a second `uniform` row.
- **UniformCurve**: $1$, with no parameters (1104–1124). No separate `uniform` function is defined.
- **DeclineLogistic / `declplogis(mean, slope, sel_coeffs)`**: $1/[1+\operatorname{mfexp}(slope(x-mean))]$, overridden at indices `x.indexmin()` through `sel_coeffs.indexmax()` by $\operatorname{mfexp}(sel\_coeffs_i)$ (283–291, 519–542). Table 1’s `declplogistic` is not the implemented name.
- **ParameterPerClass / `nonparametric(selparms)`**: $s_i=\operatorname{mfexp}(selparms_i)$ for every index of `x` (294–316, 1063–1077).
- **SelectivityCoefficients / `coefficients(sel_coeffs)`**: $s_i=\operatorname{mfexp}(sel\_coeffs_i)$ before the last coefficient index; that last value is repeated through the end of `x` (963–988, 1005–1019). Table 1’s `selcoffs` is not defined; despite the comment, this is exponential, **not logit**, and is not inherently bounded by 1.
- **AscNormal / `asc_normal(sL, z1)`**: $(1-J)G(x;z1,sL)+J$, where $J=L(5(x-z1))$ (169–177, 590–609).
- **DoubleNormal / `pdubnorm(sL, s50, sR)`**: $[(1-J)G_L+J][JG_R+(1-J)]$, with both Gaussians centered at `s50` and $J=L(5(x-s50))$ (193–203, 657–680).
- **DoubleNormal4 / `pdubnorm4(ascWdZ, ascPkZ, dscWdZ, dscPkZ)`**: $[A G_A+(1-A)][D G_D+(1-D)]$, where $A=L(-5(x-ascPkZ))$, $D=L(5(x-dscPkZ))$, and each Gaussian uses its corresponding peak and width (220–232, 817–844). These are smooth joins, not exact piecewise plateaus.
- **DoubleNormal4a / `pdubnorm4a(ascWdZ, ascPkZ, dscWdZ, sclInc, fsZ)`**: the preceding formula with $dscPkZ=ascPkZ+sclInc(fsZ-ascPkZ)$ (253–267, 735–766). `fsZ` is an additional input, normally fixed; nonnegative `sclInc` is documented, not enforced.
- **DoubleNormal6 / `pDubnorm6`**: listed only in Table 1 (line 42); neither definition nor parameter order occurs in this file. No matching implementation was found in the repository or available Git history, so its exact formula cannot be determined.
- **StackedLogistic / `plogis(omega, mean1, sd1, mean2, sd2)`**: $\omega L((x-mean1)/sd1)+(1-\omega)L((x-mean2)/sd2)$ (897–899, 925–930). The repeated `plogis` table entry means two calls to the *same* function, not another overload.
- **SelectivitySpline `(yvals, xvals)`**: calculate cubic-spline second derivatives with zero first derivatives at both endpoints, interpolate the knot values to $z(x)$, then return $\operatorname{mfexp}(z)/(1+\exp(z))$ (1222–1255, 1271–1283). Thus knot values are on the logit scale. `gsm_splines.hpp` is included at line 54 but absent from `Cpp`, so interpolation details and extrapolation cannot be verified here; the differing `T`/`T1` method signature also raises a base-class override ambiguity (81–83, 1203–1204, 1271).

Most `logSelexMeanOne` methods subtract $\log(\operatorname{mean}(s))$ from $\log(s)$; **LogisticCurve95** and **ParameterPerClass** leave that subtraction commented out (433–437, 1096–1100).

`Cpp/` is untracked, and the available history has no selectivity implementation matching the missing names. The tracked selectivity documentation section is empty (`quarto/q02c_FleetDynamics.qmd:99–102`).
</SUBAGENT>

Values of `x` outside the\n#' (0,1) range generate NaNs.\n#' @export\n#'\nlogit\u003c-function(x){\n  y = log(x/(1-x));\n  names(y) = names(x);\n  return(y);\n}\n\n#' @title Calculate a 0-symmetric logit function\n#' @description Function to calculate a logit function centered on 0\n#' @param x - numeric vector with values from -1.0 to 1.0 at which to calculate the symmetric logit function\n#' @return a numeric vector with the same size and names (if any) as `x`\n#' @details This is the inverse to `symlogistic` (see examples)\n#' @examplesIf FALSE\n#' ##--R context\n#' x = seq(-0.99,0.99,0.01);\n#' y = symlogit(x);\n#' head(y);\n#' xp = symlogistic(y) - x\n#' ##--RTMB context\n#' x = RTMB::AD(seq(-0.49,0.49,0.01),force=TRUE);\n#' y = symlogit(x);\n#' head(y)\n#' @export\n#'\nsymlogit\u003c-function(x){\n  y = (x+1)/2.0;\n  y = log(y/(1-y));\n  names(y) = names(x);\n  return(y)\n};\n\n#' @title Calculate a 0-symmetric logistic function between -1 and 1\n#' @description Function to calculate a logistic function centered on 0\n#' @param x - numeric vector at which to calculate the symmetric logistic function\n#' @return a numeric vector with the same size and names (if any) as `x`\n#' @details This is the inverse to `symlogit` (see examples)\n#' @examplesIf FALSE\n#' ##--R context\n#' x = seq(-5,5,0.1);\n#' y = symlogistic(x);\n#' head(y);\n#' xp = symlogit(y) - x\n#' ##--RTMB context\n#' x = RTMB::AD(seq(-5,5,0.1),force=TRUE);\n#' y = symlogistic(x);\n#' head(y)\n#' @export\n#'\nsymlogistic\u003c-function(x){\n  y = 2.0*exp(x)/(1+exp(x))-1.0;\n  names(y) = names(x);\n  return(y);\n}\n\n"},"elapsedMs":5}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="call_TohsXQxI0zqHqM7KAtl4qjjJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"apply_patch","input":{"patch":"*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: R/Functions-slectivity.R\n+#' Logistic selectivity\n+#'\n+#' Ca...[truncated, total 9576 chars]"},"caption":"R/Functions-slectivity.R","captionLink":"R/Functions-slectivity.R","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"--- /dev/null\n+++ R/Functions-slectivity.R\n@@ -0,0 +1,295 @@\n+#' Logistic selectivity\n+#'\n+#' Calculates logistic selectivity. With two parameters, this implements\n+#' `LogisticCurve`; with five parameters, it implements `StackedLogistic`.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `c(mean, sd)` for a logistic curve or\n+#'   `c(omega, mean1, sd1, mean2, sd2)` for stacked logistic selectivity.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\n+#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10))\n+plogis \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) == 2L) {\n+    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n+  } else if (length(p) == 5L) {\n+    first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n+    second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n+    result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n+  } else {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 2 or 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n+#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' plogis95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\n+plogis95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])))\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Selectivity coefficients\n+#'\n+#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n+#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' selcoffs(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\n+selcoffs \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z))\n+  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p)\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Nonparametric selectivity\n+#'\n+#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n+#'   for each element of `z`.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' nonparametric(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9)))\n+nonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n+    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  return(exp(p))\n+}\n+\n+#' Uniform or one-parameter logistic selectivity\n+#'\n+#' With no parameters, returns uniform selectivity. With one slope parameter,\n+#' calculates `LogisticCurveOne`, for which selectivity at the final size is\n+#' 0.999.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `numeric(0)` for uniform selectivity\n+#'   or a single slope parameter for one-parameter logistic selectivity.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = numeric(0))\n+#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\n+uniform \u003c- function(z, p = numeric(0)) {\n+  if (length(p) == 0L) {\n+    result \u003c- 1 + 0 * z\n+  } else if (length(p) == 1L) {\n+    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n+  } else {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 0 or 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Declining logistic selectivity\n+#'\n+#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n+#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n+#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' declplogistic(\n+#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n+#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n+#' )\n+declplogistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n+      call. = FALSE\n+    )\n+  }\n+\n+  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n+  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n+  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n+    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)\n+    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L])\n+  }\n+\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n+#'   descending width, in that order.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' pdubnorm(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\n+pdubnorm \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n+  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2)\n+  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n+  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n+    (join * descending + (1 - join))\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n+#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' pdubnorm4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\n+pdubnorm4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4])\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n+#'\n+#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n+#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n+#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n+#'   order.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' pdubnorm4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\n+pdubnorm4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2])\n+  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak)\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+double_normal_plateau \u003c- function(z, ascending_width, ascending_peak,\n+                                  descending_width, descending_peak) {\n+  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2)\n+  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2)\n+  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)))\n+  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)))\n+  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n+    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join))\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n+#'   selection, in that order.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\n+asc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n+  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n+  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n+#'\n+#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n+#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n+#' transformation.\n+#'\n+#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n+#' @param p Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n+#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n+#'\n+#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n+#' @export\n+#'\n+#' @examples\n+#' spline_cubic_val(\n+#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n+#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n+#' )\n+spline_cubic_val \u003c- function(z, p) {\n+  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n+    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L\n+  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)]\n+  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)]\n+  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n+    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n+  }\n+\n+  interpolator \u003c- stats::splinefun(\n+    x = knots,\n+    y = values,\n+    method = \"fmm\"\n+  )\n+  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolator(z)))\n+  return(result)\n+}\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="call_hv6gd6X5Ry2Q6jxBU9Tdgf3R"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"apply_patch","input":{"patch":"*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: R/Functions-slectivity.R\n@@\n-  interpolator \u003c- stats::spl...[truncated, total 2982 chars]"},"caption":"2 file operations","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"--- R/Functions-slectivity.R\n+++ R/Functions-slectivity.R\n@@ -285,11 +285,47 @@\n     stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n   }\n \n-  interpolator \u003c- stats::splinefun(\n-    x = knots,\n-    y = values,\n-    method = \"fmm\"\n+  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values)\n+  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE)\n+  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L)\n+  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval]\n+  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width\n+  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width\n+  interpolated \u003c- left_weight * values[interval] +\n+    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n+    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) * second_derivatives[interval] +\n+      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n+        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) * width^2 / 6\n+  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated))\n+  return(result)\n+}\n+\n+clamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n+  number_knots \u003c- length(x)\n+  widths \u003c- diff(x)\n+  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths\n+  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots)\n+  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots)\n+\n+  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1])\n+  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1]\n+\n+  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n+    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n+      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n+        widths[i - 1L],\n+        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n+        widths[i]\n+      )\n+      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L])\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n+    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n+    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n   )\n-  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolator(z)))\n+  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L]\n+  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side)\n   return(result)\n }\n\n--- /dev/null\n+++ tests/testthat/test-Functions-slectivity.R\n@@ -0,0 +1,30 @@\n+testthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n+  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n+\n+  testthat::expect_equal(plogis(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n+  testthat::expect_equal(plogis95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n+  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z), rep(1, length(z)))\n+  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z, 0.2)[length(z)], 0.999)\n+  testthat::expect_equal(nonparametric(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5, 1))), c(0.2, 0.5, 1))\n+  testthat::expect_equal(\n+    selcoffs(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5))),\n+    c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n+  )\n+})\n+\n+testthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n+  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n+  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm4(c(50, 70), c(10, 50, 15, 70)), c(1, 1))\n+  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm4a(c(50, 75), c(10, 50, 15, 0.5, 100)), c(1, 1))\n+  testthat::expect_equal(asc_normal(50, c(10, 50)), 1)\n+})\n+\n+testthat::test_that(\"spline selectivity interpolates knot values\", {\n+  knots \u003c- c(20, 50, 80)\n+  selectivity \u003c- c(0.1, 0.8, 0.4)\n+\n+  testthat::expect_equal(\n+    spline_cubic_val(knots, c(knots, stats::qlogis(selectivity))),\n+    selectivity\n+  )\n+})\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_JSr60pxEKatK0ZrR5Ck2dK7Z"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 10.4KB, 342 lines, showing lines 1-342]\n1\t#' Logistic selectivity\n2\t#'\n3\t#' Calcula...[truncated, total 11913 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 10.4KB, 342 lines, showing lines 1-342]\n#' Logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates logistic selectivity. With two parameters, this implements\n#' `LogisticCurve`; with five parameters, it implements `StackedLogistic`.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `c(mean, sd)` for a logistic curve or\n#'   `c(omega, mean1, sd1, mean2, sd2)` for stacked logistic selectivity.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\n#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10))\nplogis \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) == 2L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n  } else if (length(p) == 5L) {\n    first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n    second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n    result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n  } else {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2 or 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' plogis95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nplogis95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' selcoffs(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nselcoffs \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z))\n  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p)\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Nonparametric selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n#'   for each element of `z`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' nonparametric(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9)))\nnonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(exp(p))\n}\n\n#' Uniform or one-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' With no parameters, returns uniform selectivity. With one slope parameter,\n#' calculates `LogisticCurveOne`, for which selectivity at the final size is\n#' 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `numeric(0)` for uniform selectivity\n#'   or a single slope parameter for one-parameter logistic selectivity.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = numeric(0))\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nuniform \u003c- function(z, p = numeric(0)) {\n  if (length(p) == 0L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 + 0 * z\n  } else if (length(p) == 1L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n  } else {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 0 or 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' declplogistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\ndeclplogistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    )\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)\n    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L])\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n#'   descending width, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\npdubnorm \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n    (join * descending + (1 - join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\npdubnorm4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4])\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\npdubnorm4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2])\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak)\n  return(result)\n}\n\ndouble_normal_plateau \u003c- function(\n  z,\n  ascending_width,\n  ascending_peak,\n  descending_width,\n  descending_peak\n) {\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2)\n  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)))\n  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)))\n  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n#'   selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\nasc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n#'\n#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n#' transformation.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' spline_cubic_val(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n#' )\nspline_cubic_val \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L\n  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)]\n  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)]\n  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values)\n  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE)\n  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L)\n  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval]\n  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width\n  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width\n  interpolated \u003c- left_weight *\n    values[interval] +\n    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) *\n      second_derivatives[interval] +\n      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) *\n      width^2 /\n      6\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated))\n  return(result)\n}\n\nclamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n  number_knots \u003c- length(x)\n  widths \u003c- diff(x)\n  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths\n  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots)\n  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots)\n\n  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1])\n  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1]\n\n  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n        widths[i - 1L],\n        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n        widths[i]\n      )\n      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L])\n    }\n  }\n\n  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n  )\n  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L]\n  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side)\n  return(result)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_jCauZfYJhTrDAnoIp5WeOjLk"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 1.1KB, 34 lines, showing lines 1-34]\n1\ttestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions r...[truncated, total 1247 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 1.1KB, 34 lines, showing lines 1-34]\ntestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(plogis(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n  testthat::expect_equal(plogis95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z), rep(1, length(z)))\n  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z, 0.2)[length(z)], 0.999)\n  testthat::expect_equal(nonparametric(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5, 1))), c(0.2, 0.5, 1))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    selcoffs(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n  )\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm4(c(50, 70), c(10, 50, 15, 70)), c(1, 1))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    pdubnorm4a(c(50, 75), c(10, 50, 15, 0.5, 100)),\n    c(1, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(asc_normal(50, c(10, 50)), 1)\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"spline selectivity interpolates knot values\", {\n  knots \u003c- c(20, 50, 80)\n  selectivity \u003c- c(0.1, 0.8, 0.4)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    spline_cubic_val(knots, c(knots, stats::qlogis(selectivity))),\n    selectivity\n  )\n})\n"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_LZn6BObiVSItIA1KHzvRlnZM"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 10.4KB, 342 lines, showing lines 1-342]\n1\t#' Logistic selectivity\n2\t#'\n3\t#' Calcula...[truncated, total 11913 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 10.4KB, 342 lines, showing lines 1-342]\n#' Logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates logistic selectivity. With two parameters, this implements\n#' `LogisticCurve`; with five parameters, it implements `StackedLogistic`.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `c(mean, sd)` for a logistic curve or\n#'   `c(omega, mean1, sd1, mean2, sd2)` for stacked logistic selectivity.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\n#' plogis(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10))\nplogis \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) == 2L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n  } else if (length(p) == 5L) {\n    first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n    second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n    result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n  } else {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2 or 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' plogis95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nplogis95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' selcoffs(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nselcoffs \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z))\n  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p)\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Nonparametric selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n#'   for each element of `z`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' nonparametric(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9)))\nnonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(exp(p))\n}\n\n#' Uniform or one-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' With no parameters, returns uniform selectivity. With one slope parameter,\n#' calculates `LogisticCurveOne`, for which selectivity at the final size is\n#' 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `numeric(0)` for uniform selectivity\n#'   or a single slope parameter for one-parameter logistic selectivity.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = numeric(0))\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nuniform \u003c- function(z, p = numeric(0)) {\n  if (length(p) == 0L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 + 0 * z\n  } else if (length(p) == 1L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n  } else {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 0 or 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' declplogistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\ndeclplogistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    )\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)\n    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L])\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n#'   descending width, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\npdubnorm \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n    (join * descending + (1 - join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\npdubnorm4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4])\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' pdubnorm4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\npdubnorm4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2])\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak)\n  return(result)\n}\n\ndouble_normal_plateau \u003c- function(\n  z,\n  ascending_width,\n  ascending_peak,\n  descending_width,\n  descending_peak\n) {\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2)\n  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)))\n  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)))\n  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n#'   selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\nasc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n#'\n#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n#' transformation.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' spline_cubic_val(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n#' )\nspline_cubic_val \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L\n  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)]\n  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)]\n  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values)\n  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE)\n  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L)\n  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval]\n  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width\n  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width\n  interpolated \u003c- left_weight *\n    values[interval] +\n    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) *\n      second_derivatives[interval] +\n      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) *\n      width^2 /\n      6\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated))\n  return(result)\n}\n\nclamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n  number_knots \u003c- length(x)\n  widths \u003c- diff(x)\n  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths\n  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots)\n  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots)\n\n  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1])\n  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1]\n\n  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n        widths[i - 1L],\n        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n        widths[i]\n      )\n      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L])\n    }\n  }\n\n  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n  )\n  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L]\n  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side)\n  return(result)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_LCPLmLkcvD8KQ1bnFUIpSdAi"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 10.8KB, 366 lines, showing lines 105-174]\n105\t#' @examples\n106\t#' sel_nonparametric...[truncated, total 2425 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 10.8KB, 366 lines, showing lines 105-174]\n#' @examples\n#' sel_nonparametric(\n#'   z = seq(20, 60, by = 10),\n#'   p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9))\n#' )\nsel_nonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(exp(p))\n}\n\n#' Uniform or one-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' With no parameters, returns uniform selectivity. With one slope parameter,\n#' calculates `LogisticCurveOne`, for which selectivity at the final size is\n#' 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric parameter vector. Use `numeric(0)` for uniform selectivity\n#'   or a single slope parameter for one-parameter logistic selectivity.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = numeric(0))\n#' uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nuniform \u003c- function(z, p = numeric(0)) {\n  if (length(p) == 0L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 + 0 * z\n  } else if (length(p) == 1L) {\n    result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n  } else {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 0 or 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' declplogistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\ndeclplogistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    )\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_kLrnhGHXjVZK1oPN46WMDSI8"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 1.2KB, 41 lines, showing lines 1-41]\n1\ttestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions r...[truncated, total 1419 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 1.2KB, 41 lines, showing lines 1-41]\ntestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_stacked_logistic(z, c(0.25, 50, 10, 50, 10)),\n    sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z), rep(1, length(z)))\n  testthat::expect_equal(uniform(z, 0.2)[length(z)], 0.999)\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_nonparametric(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5, 1))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_coefficients(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n  )\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm4(c(50, 70), c(10, 50, 15, 70)), c(1, 1))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    pdubnorm4a(c(50, 75), c(10, 50, 15, 0.5, 100)),\n    c(1, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(asc_normal(50, c(10, 50)), 1)\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"spline selectivity interpolates knot values\", {\n  knots \u003c- c(20, 50, 80)\n  selectivity \u003c- c(0.1, 0.8, 0.4)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    spline_cubic_val(knots, c(knots, stats::qlogis(selectivity))),\n    selectivity\n  )\n})\n"},"elapsedMs":8}</script></tool-result></div>

Refactored `uniform()` into:

- `sel_uniform(z, p)`: returns `p[1]` for every `z`.
- `sel_logistic1(z, p)`: computes one-parameter logistic selectivity.

Documentation, exports, and tests were updated. All 12 tests pass.

## User

rename declplogistic, pdubnorm, pdubnorm4, pdubnorm4a to sel_declining_logistic, sel_dbl_normal3, sel_dbl_normal4, sel_dbl_normal4a respectively.

<div><tool-result call-id="call_XRgNWeipqab7Tr7Pr6ea4e1Z"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 1.2KB, 41 lines, showing lines 1-41]\n1\ttestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions r...[truncated, total 1436 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 1.2KB, 41 lines, showing lines 1-41]\ntestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_stacked_logistic(z, c(0.25, 50, 10, 50, 10)),\n    sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_uniform(z, 0.8), rep(0.8, length(z)))\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic1(z, 0.2)[length(z)], 0.999)\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_nonparametric(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5, 1))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_coefficients(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n  )\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n  testthat::expect_equal(pdubnorm4(c(50, 70), c(10, 50, 15, 70)), c(1, 1))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    pdubnorm4a(c(50, 75), c(10, 50, 15, 0.5, 100)),\n    c(1, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(asc_normal(50, c(10, 50)), 1)\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"spline selectivity interpolates knot values\", {\n  knots \u003c- c(20, 50, 80)\n  selectivity \u003c- c(0.1, 0.8, 0.4)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    spline_cubic_val(knots, c(knots, stats::qlogis(selectivity))),\n    selectivity\n  )\n})\n"},"elapsedMs":3}</script></tool-result></div>

Place the closing curly brace on a new line after the enclosed code.\n7. Indent code enclosed by loops and conditional statements two spaces beyond the statement declaration.\n8. Always end a code statement with \";\".\n\n## RTMB functions\n\nFunctions that implement autodifferention functionality found in the RTMB package should:\n\n1. have \"rtmbFunction\" as a roxygen
