using 'AD(vector(\"numeric\",n)) where \"n\" is the vector length\n5. use the Matrix package to create and manipulate matrix objects\n6. always use Matrix::expm rather than expm::expm for matrix exponentiation.\n\nTests for each function (say \"fcn(x,y,z,a)\") should create a scaffold that: \n\n1. starts the testing function by calling \"library(RTMB)\"\n2. assigns values to input variables (e.g., x,y,z)\n3. calculates fcn based on selected values for inputs in \"normal\" R, e.g.\n\n   valsR = fcn(x,y,z,a);\n   \n4. identifies which function variables are \"data\" from the associated man which function variables are \"param\"s from the associated man page \n   and creates a named list \"params\" with elements named for each variable identified as \"param\",\n   e.g. with z and a as param variables\n   \n   params = list(z=z,a=a);\n   \n6. creates a modified function \"fcn1\" (using fcn(x,y,z,a) as an example, \n   where x and y are marked as \"data\" and z and a are marked as \"param\"s) as \n   \n  fcn1 \u003c- function(data,params){\n    val = fcn(data$x,data$y,params$z,params$a);\n    return(val);\n  }\n7. creates an \"enclosing\"\" function \"encf\" as \n  \n  encf \u003c- function(f,data){function(params){f(data,params)}}\n  \n8. calculates the number of parameters N as \n\n  N \u003c-sum(sapply(params,length))\n  \n9. creates an RTMB \"tape\" of the modified function\n\n  F \u003c- MakeTape(encf(fcn1, data), numeric(N));\n  \n10. evaluates F\n\n  p = as.numeric(unlist(params));\n  \n  valRTMB \u003c- F(p)\n  \n11. asserts that valR = valRTMB\n\n## <div><tool-result call-id="call_XBEbDCVTAbxjnD05nWXWnDNu"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 11.2KB, 383 lines, showing lines 1-55]\n1\t#' Logistic selectivity\n2\t#'\n3\t#' Calculat...[truncated, total 1837 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 11.2KB, 383 lines, showing lines 1-55]\n#' Logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates logistic selectivity using the `LogisticCurve` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the mean and standard deviation, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\nsel_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Stacked logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates a weighted average of two logistic selectivity curves using the\n#' `StackedLogistic` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the weight, first mean, first standard\n#'   deviation, second mean, and second standard deviation, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_stacked_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10)\n#' )\nsel_stacked_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n  second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n  result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_mBC5ncGufoMBCYszd0UGScgv"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 1.3KB, 44 lines, showing lines 1-44]\n1\ttestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions r...[truncated, total 1480 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 1.3KB, 44 lines, showing lines 1-44]\ntestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_stacked_logistic(z, c(0.25, 50, 10, 50, 10)),\n    sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_uniform(z, 0.8), rep(0.8, length(z)))\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic1(z, 0.2)[length(z)], 0.999)\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_nonparametric(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5, 1))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_coefficients(z, log(c(0.2, 0.5))),\n    c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n  )\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_dbl_normal3(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_dbl_normal4(c(50, 70), c(10, 50, 15, 70)),\n    c(1, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_dbl_normal4a(c(50, 75), c(10, 50, 15, 0.5, 100)),\n    c(1, 1)\n  )\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_asc_normal(50, c(10, 50)), 1)\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"spline selectivity interpolates knot values\", {\n  knots \u003c- c(20, 50, 80)\n  selectivity \u003c- c(0.1, 0.8, 0.4)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n    sel_cubic_spline(knots, c(knots, stats::qlogis(selectivity))),\n    selectivity\n  )\n})\n"},"elapsedMs":8}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_E8xAY9KN0xjUkLul5KETjY06"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 1.9KB, 73 lines, showing lines 1-35]\n1\ttestthat::test_that(\"sel_logistic works with...[truncated, total 1012 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 1.9KB, 73 lines, showing lines 1-35]\ntestthat::test_that(\"sel_logistic works with RTMB autodifferentiation\", {\n  library(RTMB);\n\n  z \u003c- seq(20, 100, by = 10);\n  p \u003c- c(50, 8);\n  valsR \u003c- sel_logistic(z, p);\n\n  data \u003c- list(z = z);\n  params \u003c- list(p = p);\n\n  fcn1 \u003c- function(data, params) {\n    val \u003c- sel_logistic(data$z, params$p);\n    return(val);\n  };\n\n  encf \u003c- function(f, data) {\n    function(params) {\n      f(data, list(p = params));\n    };\n  };\n\n  N \u003c- sum(sapply(params, length));\n  F \u003c- MakeTape(encf(fcn1, data), numeric(N));\n  p \u003c- as.numeric(unlist(params));\n  valRTMB \u003c- F(p);\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(as.numeric(valRTMB), valsR);\n});\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"selectivity functions reproduce defining values\", {\n  z \u003c- c(40, 50, 70)\n\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic(z, c(50, 10))[2], 0.5)\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_logistic95(z, c(50, 70))[2:3], c(0.5, 0.95))\n  testthat::expect_equal("},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_dbd2LViIGPcbMpyTKLAIVPcK"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 3.5KB, 100 lines, showing lines 1-100]\n1\t# Project Instructions\n2\t\n3\t## R code styl...[truncated, total 3884 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 3.5KB, 100 lines, showing lines 1-100]\n# Project Instructions\n\n## R code style\n\n1. Always use spaces in R code. Do not use tabs.\n2. Qualify functions with their package names using `package::function()`, except for functions from the `base` and `stats` packages.\n3. Use two spaces for each indentation level.\n4. When defining a function, place the opening curly brace on the same line as the function signature. Place the closing curly brace on a new line after the function body unless the entire function body is written on one line.\n5. Functions that return values must use an explicit `return()` statement.\n6. For `for`, `while`, and `if` statements, place the opening curly brace at the end of the loop or test declaration. Place the closing curly brace on a new line after the enclosed code.\n7. Indent code enclosed by loops and conditional statements two spaces beyond the statement declaration.\n8. Always end a code statement with \";\".\n\n## RTMB functions\n\nFunctions that implement autodifferention functionality found in the RTMB package should:\n\n1. have \"rtmbFunction\" as a roxygen keyword\n2. indicate which function variables are \"data\" and which are \"param\"'s in roxygen @param declaration\n3. include the following code lines as the first executable lines in the function\n    - \"[\u003c-\"  \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n    - \"c\"    \u003c- ADoverload(\"[c\");\n    - \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[diag\u003c-\");\n4. preallocate (if possible) AD vectors using 'AD(vector(\"numeric\",n)) where \"n\" is the vector length\n5. use the Matrix package to create and manipulate matrix objects\n6. always use Matrix::expm rather than expm::expm for matrix exponentiation.\n\nTests for each function (say \"fcn(x,y,z,a)\") should create a scaffold that: \n\n1. starts the testing function by calling \"library(RTMB)\"\n2. assigns values to input variables (e.g., x,y,z)\n3. calculates fcn based on selected values for inputs in \"normal\" R, e.g.\n\n   valsR = fcn(x,y,z,a);\n   \n4. identifies which function variables are \"data\" from the associated man page \n   and creates a list named \"data\" with elements named for each variable identified as \"data\", \n   e.g. with x, y the \"data\" variables\n   \n   data = list(x=x,y=y);\n   \n5. identifies which function variables are \"param\"s from the associated man page \n   and creates a named list \"params\" with elements named for each variable identified as \"param\",\n   e.g. with z and a as param variables\n   \n   params = list(z=z,a=a);\n   \n6. creates a modified function \"fcn1\" (using fcn(x,y,z,a) as an example, \n   where x and y are marked as \"data\" and z and a are marked as \"param\"s) as \n   \n  fcn1 \u003c- function(data,params){\n    val = fcn(data$x,data$y,params$z,params$a);\n    return(val);\n  }\n7. creates an \"enclosing\"\" function \"encf\" as \n  \n  encf \u003c- function(f,data){function(params){f(data,params)}}\n  \n8. calculates the number of parameters N as \n\n  N \u003c-sum(sapply(params,length))\n  \n9. creates an RTMB \"tape\" of the modified function\n\n  F \u003c- MakeTape(encf(fcn1, data), numeric(N));\n  \n10. evaluates F\n\n  p = as.numeric(unlist(params));\n  \n  valRTMB \u003c- F(p)\n  \n11. asserts that valR = valRTMB\n\n## Markdown/Quarto style\n\n1. in a markdown cell, start code indented once as in Example\n\nExample:\n\n```r\n  calculate_mean \u003c- function(x) {\n    if (length(x) == 0) {\n      return(NA_real_);\n    }\n\n    values \u003c- dplyr::filter(x, !is.na(value));\n\n    for (i in seq_len(nrow(values))) {\n      values$value[i] \u003c- as.numeric(values$value[i]);\n    }\n\n    return(mean(values$value));\n  }\n```\n\n## Repository website\n\n1. Always use quarto/_quarto.yml to create repository website on gh-pages branch\n"},"elapsedMs":8}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_QoZuIP2SpVlwcwd2QzTFZ3DU"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 11.3KB, 387 lines, showing lines 1-387]\n1\t#' Logistic selectivity\n2\t#'\n3\t#' Calcula...[truncated, total 13061 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 11.3KB, 387 lines, showing lines 1-387]\n#' Logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates logistic selectivity using the GMACS `LogisticCurve` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the mean and standard deviation,\n#'   in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\nsel_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\")\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\")\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\")\n\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Stacked logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates a weighted average of two logistic selectivity curves using the\n#' GMACS `StackedLogistic` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the weight, first mean, first standard\n#'   deviation, second mean, and second standard deviation, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_stacked_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10)\n#' )\nsel_stacked_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n  second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n  result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nsel_logistic95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_coefficients(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nsel_coefficients \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z))\n  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p)\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Nonparametric selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n#'   for each element of `z`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_nonparametric(\n#'   z = seq(20, 60, by = 10),\n#'   p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9))\n#' )\nsel_nonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(exp(p))\n}\n\n#' Uniform selectivity\n#'\n#' Returns the parameter value for every size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the uniform selectivity value.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.8)\nsel_uniform \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- p[1] + 0 * z\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' One-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates GMACS `LogisticCurveOne` selectivity from one slope parameter. The\n#' selectivity at the final size is 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic slope.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic1(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nsel_logistic1 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_declining_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\nsel_declining_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    )\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)\n    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L])\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n#'   descending width, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal3(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\nsel_dbl_normal3 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n    (join * descending + (1 - join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\nsel_dbl_normal4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4])\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\nsel_dbl_normal4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2])\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak)\n  return(result)\n}\n\ndouble_normal_plateau \u003c- function(\n  z,\n  ascending_width,\n  ascending_peak,\n  descending_width,\n  descending_peak\n) {\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2)\n  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)))\n  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)))\n  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n#'   selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\nsel_asc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n#'\n#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n#' transformation.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction, selectivity\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_cubic_spline(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n#' )\nsel_cubic_spline \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L\n  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)]\n  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)]\n  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values)\n  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE)\n  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L)\n  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval]\n  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width\n  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width\n  interpolated \u003c- left_weight *\n    values[interval] +\n    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) *\n      second_derivatives[interval] +\n      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) *\n      width^2 /\n      6\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated))\n  return(result)\n}\n\nclamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n  number_knots \u003c- length(x)\n  widths \u003c- diff(x)\n  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths\n  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots)\n  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots)\n\n  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1])\n  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1]\n\n  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n        widths[i - 1L],\n        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n        widths[i]\n      )\n      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L])\n    }\n  }\n\n  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n  )\n  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L]\n  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side)\n  return(result)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

Always use spaces in R code. Do not use tabs.\n2. Qualify functions with their package names using `package::function()`, except for functions from the `base` and `stats` packages.\n3. Use two spaces for each indentation level.\n4. When defining a function, place the opening curly brace on the same line as the function signature. Place the closing curly brace on a new line after the function body unless the entire function body is written on one line.\n5. Functions that return values must use an explicit `return()` statement.\n6. For `for`, `while`, and `if` statements, place the opening curly brace at the end of the loop or test declaration. Place the closing curly brace on a new line after the enclosed code.\n7. Indent code enclosed by loops and conditional statements two spaces beyond the statement declaration.\n8. Always end a code statement with \";\".\n\n## RTMB functions\n\nFunctions that implement autodifferention functionality found in the RTMB package should:\n\n1. have \"rtmbFunction\" as a roxygen keyword\n2. indicate which function variables are \"data\" and which are \"param\"'s in roxygen @param declaration\n3. include the following code lines as the first executable lines in the function\n    - \"[\u003c-\"  \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n    - \"c\"    \u003c- ADoverload(\"[c\");\n    - \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[diag\u003c-\");\n4. preallocate (if possible) AD vectors using 'AD(vector(\"numeric\",n)) where \"n\" is the vector length\n5. use the Matrix package to create and manipulate matrix objects\n6. always use Matrix::expm rather than expm::expm for matrix exponentiation.\n\nTests for each function (say \"fcn(x,y,z,a)\") should create a scaffold that: \n\n1. starts the testing function by calling \"library(RTMB)\"\n2. assigns values to input variables (e.g., x,y,z)\n3. calculates fcn based on selected values for inputs in \"normal\" R, e.g.\n\n   valsR = fcn(x,y,z,a);\n   \n4. identifies which function variables are \"data\" from the associated man page \n   and creates a list named \"data\" with elements named for each variable identified as \"data\", \n   e.g. with x, y the \"data\" variables\n   \n   data = list(x=x,y=y);\n   \n5. identifies which function variables are \"param\"s from the associated man page \n   and creates a named list \"params\" with elements named for each variable identified as \"param\",\n   e.g. with z and a as param variables\n   \n   params = list(z=z,a=a);\n   \n6. creates a modified function \"fcn1\" (using fcn(x,y,z,a) as an example, \n   where x and y are marked as \"data\" and z and a are marked as \"param\"s) as \n   \n  fcn1 \u003c- function(data,params){\n    val = fcn(data$x,data$y,params$z,params$a);\n    return(val);\n  }\n7. creates an \"enclosing\"\" function \"encf\" as \n  \n  encf \u003c- function(f,data){function(params){f(data,params)}}\n  \n8. calculates the number of parameters N as \n\n  N \u003c-sum(sapply(params,length))\n  \n9. creates an RTMB \"tape\" of the modified function\n\n  F \u003c- MakeTape(encf(fcn1, data), numeric(N));\n  \n10. evaluates F\n\n  p = as.numeric(unlist(params));\n  \n  valRTMB \u003c- F(p)\n  \n11. asserts that valR = valRTMB\n\n## Markdown/Quarto style\n\n1. in a markdown cell, start code indented once as in Example\n\nExample:\n\n```r\n  calculate_mean \u003c- function(x) {\n    if (length(x) == 0) {\n      return(NA_real_);\n    }\n\n    values \u003c- dplyr::filter(x, !is.na(value));\n\n    for (i in seq_len(nrow(values))) {\n      values$value[i] \u003c- as.numeric(values$value[i]);\n    }\n\n    return(mean(values$value));\n  }\n```\n\n## Repository website\n\n1. <div><tool-result call-id="call_lzUu3mcxNwGphsOGGr4aSuCU"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 11.3KB, 387 lines, showing lines 1-387]\n1\t#' Logistic selectivity\n2\t#'\n3\t#' Calcula...[truncated, total 13061 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 11.3KB, 387 lines, showing lines 1-387]\n#' Logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates logistic selectivity using the GMACS `LogisticCurve` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the mean and standard deviation,\n#'   in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 8))\nsel_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\")\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\")\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\")\n\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[1]) / p[2]))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Stacked logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates a weighted average of two logistic selectivity curves using the\n#' GMACS `StackedLogistic` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the weight, first mean, first standard\n#'   deviation, second mean, and second standard deviation, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_stacked_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10)\n#' )\nsel_stacked_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]))\n  second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]))\n  result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nsel_logistic95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_coefficients(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nsel_coefficients \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z))\n  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p)\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Nonparametric selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n#'   for each element of `z`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_nonparametric(\n#'   z = seq(20, 60, by = 10),\n#'   p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9))\n#' )\nsel_nonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  return(exp(p))\n}\n\n#' Uniform selectivity\n#'\n#' Returns the parameter value for every size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the uniform selectivity value.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.8)\nsel_uniform \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- p[1] + 0 * z\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' One-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates GMACS `LogisticCurveOne` selectivity from one slope parameter. The\n#' selectivity at the final size is 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic slope.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic1(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nsel_logistic1 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_declining_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\nsel_declining_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    )\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])))\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients)\n    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L])\n  }\n\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n#'   descending width, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal3(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\nsel_dbl_normal3 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n    (join * descending + (1 - join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\nsel_dbl_normal4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4])\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\nsel_dbl_normal4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2])\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak)\n  return(result)\n}\n\ndouble_normal_plateau \u003c- function(\n  z,\n  ascending_width,\n  ascending_peak,\n  descending_width,\n  descending_peak\n) {\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2)\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2)\n  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)))\n  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)))\n  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join))\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n#'   selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\nsel_asc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2)\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])))\n  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join\n  return(result)\n}\n\n#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n#'\n#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n#' transformation.\n#'\n#' @param z Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction, selectivity\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_cubic_spline(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n#' )\nsel_cubic_spline \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L\n  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)]\n  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)]\n  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE)\n  }\n\n  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values)\n  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE)\n  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L)\n  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval]\n  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width\n  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width\n  interpolated \u003c- left_weight *\n    values[interval] +\n    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) *\n      second_derivatives[interval] +\n      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) *\n      width^2 /\n      6\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated))\n  return(result)\n}\n\nclamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n  number_knots \u003c- length(x)\n  widths \u003c- diff(x)\n  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths\n  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots)\n  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots)\n\n  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1])\n  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1]\n\n  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n        widths[i - 1L],\n        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n        widths[i]\n      )\n      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L])\n    }\n  }\n\n  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n  )\n  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L]\n  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side)\n  return(result)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

c(0.2, 0.5, 0.5)\n  )\n})\n\ntestthat::test_that(\"double-normal and ascending curves peak as defined\", {\n  testthat::expect_equal(sel_dbl_normal3(50, c(10, 50, 15)), 1)\n  testthat::expect_equal(\n p[1]) / p[2]));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Stacked logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates a weighted average of two logistic selectivity curves using the\n#' GMACS `StackedLogistic` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the weight, first mean, first standard\n#'   deviation, second mean, and second standard deviation, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_stacked_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10)\n#' )\nsel_stacked_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]));\n  second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]));\n  result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second;\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nsel_logistic95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_coefficients(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nsel_coefficients \u003c- function(z, p) {"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

p[1]) / p[2]));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Stacked logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates a weighted average of two logistic selectivity curves using the\n#' GMACS `StackedLogistic` parameterization.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the weight, first mean, first standard\n#'   deviation, second mean, and second standard deviation, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_stacked_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(0.6, 40, 6, 70, 10)\n#' )\nsel_stacked_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  first \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[2]) / p[3]));\n  second \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-(z - p[4]) / p[5]));\n  result \u003c- p[1] * first + (1 - p[1]) * second;\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Logistic selectivity parameterized by 50 and 95 percent selection\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing size at 50 percent selection and size at\n#'   95 percent selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic95(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(50, 70))\nsel_logistic95 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-log(19) * (z - p[1]) / (p[2] - p[1])));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Selectivity coefficients\n#'\n#' Exponentiates log-scale coefficients. If fewer coefficients than sizes are\n#' supplied, the final coefficient is repeated for the remaining sizes.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity coefficients.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_coefficients(z = seq(20, 70, by = 10), p = log(c(0.2, 0.5, 0.9)))\nsel_coefficients \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) \u003c 1L || length(p) > length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length between 1 and `length(z)`.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- rep(exp(p[length(p)]), length(z));\n  result[seq_along(p)] \u003c- exp(p);\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Nonparametric selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates one selectivity value per size from log-scale parameters.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector of log-scale selectivity parameters, with one value\n#'   for each element of `z`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_nonparametric(\n#'   z = seq(20, 60, by = 10),\n#'   p = log(c(0.1, 0.4, 0.8, 1, 0.9))\n#' )\nsel_nonparametric \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != length(z)) {\n    stop(\"`p` and `z` must have the same length.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  return(exp(p));\n};\n\n#' Uniform selectivity\n#'\n#' Returns the parameter value for every size.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the uniform selectivity value.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_uniform(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.8)\nsel_uniform \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- p[1] + 0 * z;\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' One-parameter logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates GMACS `LogisticCurveOne` selectivity from one slope parameter. The\n#' selectivity at the final size is 0.999.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the logistic slope.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_logistic1(z = seq(20, 60, by = 10), p = 0.2)\nsel_logistic1 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 1L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 1.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[1] * (-z + z[length(z)]) + log(1 / 0.999 - 1)));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Declining logistic selectivity\n#'\n#' Calculates declining logistic selectivity and replaces its first values\n#' with exponentiated log-scale coefficients.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing the logistic mean, logistic slope, and\n#'   zero or more log-scale coefficients for the first size classes.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_declining_logistic(\n#'   z = seq(20, 80, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(60, 0.12, log(c(0.1, 0.25)))\n#' )\nsel_declining_logistic \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) \u003c 2L || length(p) > length(z) + 2L) {\n    stop(\n      \"`p` must contain a mean, slope, and at most `length(z)` coefficients.\",\n      call. = FALSE\n    );\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(p[2] * (z - p[1])));\n  number_coefficients \u003c- length(p) - 2L;\n  if (number_coefficients > 0L) {\n    indices \u003c- seq_len(number_coefficients);\n    result[indices] \u003c- exp(p[indices + 2L]);\n  };\n\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Three-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing ascending width, peak size, and\n#'   descending width, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal3(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60, 18))\nsel_dbl_normal3 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 3L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 3.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2);\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[3])^2);\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])));\n  result \u003c- ((1 - join) * ascending + join) *\n    (join * descending + (1 - join));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, and descending peak size, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 75))\nsel_dbl_normal4 \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 4L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 4.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], p[4]);\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Scaled four-parameter double-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' The descending peak is calculated by scaling the distance from the ascending\n#' peak to the maximum possible size.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing ascending width, ascending peak size,\n#'   descending width, scaled increment, and maximum possible size, in that\n#'   order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_dbl_normal4a(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 50, 18, 0.5, 100))\nsel_dbl_normal4a \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 5L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 5.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  descending_peak \u003c- p[2] + p[4] * (p[5] - p[2]);\n  result \u003c- double_normal_plateau(z, p[1], p[2], p[3], descending_peak);\n  return(result);\n};\n\ndouble_normal_plateau \u003c- function(\n  z,\n  ascending_width,\n  ascending_peak,\n  descending_width,\n  descending_peak\n) {\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - ascending_peak) / ascending_width)^2);\n  descending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - descending_peak) / descending_width)^2);\n  ascending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(5 * (z - ascending_peak)));\n  descending_join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - descending_peak)));\n  result \u003c- (ascending_join * ascending + (1 - ascending_join)) *\n    (descending_join * descending + (1 - descending_join));\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Ascending-normal selectivity\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector containing ascending-limb width and size at full\n#'   selection, in that order.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_asc_normal(z = seq(20, 100, by = 10), p = c(12, 60))\nsel_asc_normal \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) != 2L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must have length 2.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  ascending \u003c- exp(-0.5 * ((z - p[2]) / p[1])^2);\n  join \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-5 * (z - p[2])));\n  result \u003c- (1 - join) * ascending + join;\n  return(result);\n};\n\n#' Cubic-spline selectivity\n#'\n#' Fits a cubic spline through logit-scale values using zero first derivatives\n#' at both endpoints, evaluates it at each size, and applies the logistic\n#' transformation.\n#'\n#' @param z data: Numeric vector of sizes.\n#' @param p param: Numeric vector formed by concatenating knot locations and the same\n#'   number of logit-scale values: `c(knots, values)`.\n#'\n#' @return A numeric vector of selectivity values with the same length as `z`.\n#' @keywords rtmbFunction, selectivity\n#' @export\n#'\n#' @examples\n#' sel_cubic_spline(\n#'   z = seq(20, 100, by = 10),\n#'   p = c(c(20, 50, 80, 100), stats::qlogis(c(0.1, 0.8, 1 - 1e-4, 0.6)))\n#' )\nsel_cubic_spline \u003c- function(z, p) {\n  \"[\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"[\u003c-\");\n  \"c\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"c\");\n  \"diag\u003c-\" \u003c- ADoverload(\"diag\u003c-\");\n\n  if (length(p) \u003c 4L || length(p) %% 2L != 0L) {\n    stop(\"`p` must contain equal-length knot and value vectors.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  number_knots \u003c- length(p) / 2L;\n  knots \u003c- p[seq_len(number_knots)];\n  values \u003c- p[number_knots + seq_len(number_knots)];\n  if (is.unsorted(knots, strictly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Knot locations in `p` must be strictly increasing.\", call. = FALSE);\n  };\n\n  second_derivatives \u003c- clamped_spline_second_derivatives(knots, values);\n  interval \u003c- findInterval(z, knots, all.inside = TRUE);\n  interval \u003c- pmin(interval, number_knots - 1L);\n  width \u003c- knots[interval + 1L] - knots[interval];\n  left_weight \u003c- (knots[interval + 1L] - z) / width;\n  right_weight \u003c- (z - knots[interval]) / width;\n  interpolated \u003c- left_weight *\n    values[interval] +\n    right_weight * values[interval + 1L] +\n    ((left_weight^3 - left_weight) *\n      second_derivatives[interval] +\n      (right_weight^3 - right_weight) *\n        second_derivatives[interval + 1L]) *\n      width^2 /\n      6;\n  result \u003c- 1 / (1 + exp(-interpolated));\n  return(result);\n};\n\nclamped_spline_second_derivatives \u003c- function(x, y) {\n  number_knots \u003c- length(x);\n  widths \u003c- diff(x);\n  slopes \u003c- diff(y) / widths;\n  system_matrix \u003c- matrix(0, nrow = number_knots, ncol = number_knots);\n  right_hand_side \u003c- numeric(number_knots);\n\n  system_matrix[1, 1:2] \u003c- c(2 * widths[1], widths[1]);\n  right_hand_side[1] \u003c- 6 * slopes[1];\n\n  if (number_knots > 2L) {\n    for (i in 2:(number_knots - 1L)) {\n      system_matrix[i, (i - 1L):(i + 1L)] \u003c- c(\n        widths[i - 1L],\n        2 * (widths[i - 1L] + widths[i]),\n        widths[i]\n      );\n      right_hand_side[i] \u003c- 6 * (slopes[i] - slopes[i - 1L]);\n    };\n  };\n\n  system_matrix[number_knots, (number_knots - 1L):number_knots] \u003c- c(\n    widths[number_knots - 1L],\n    2 * widths[number_knots - 1L]\n  );\n  right_hand_side[number_knots] \u003c- -6 * slopes[number_knots - 1L];\n  result \u003c- solve(system_matrix, right_hand_side);\n  return(result);\n};\n"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_4JA6aiVHJQSiw1wNzhSDTZH3"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 19.3KB, 495 lines, showing lines 450-495]\n450\t#' concatText(1,2,3)\n451\t#' concatTex...[truncated, total 1349 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 19.3KB, 495 lines, showing lines 450-495]\n#' concatText(1,2,3)\n#' concatText(1,NA,3)\n#' concatText(1,2,NULL,4)\n#' export\n#'\nconcatText\u003c-function(...,concat=\"+\"){\n  n = ...length();\n  str = ...elt(1);\n  ctr = 2;\n  while (ctr\u003c=n){\n    if (!is.null(...elt(ctr))) str = paste(str,concat,...elt(ctr));\n    ctr = ctr+1;\n  }\n  return(str);\n}\n\n#' @title Write dataframe to string \n#' @details Function to write a dataframe to a string.\n#' @examplesIf TRUE\n#' # example code\n#' ##--define model dimensions\n#' fleets  = c(\"TCF\",\"SCF\",\"RKF\");#--fleets\n#' years   = 2020:2025;\n#' seasons = 1:2;\n#' sexes = c(\"male\",\"female\");\n#' dfrMM = tidyr::expand_grid(f=fleets[c(1,3)],\n#'                            x=sexes);\n#' str = writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrMM);\n#' cat(str);\n#' cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrMM,delim=\",\"));\n#' @importFrom readr format_delim\n#' @export\n#' \nwriteToStr_Dataframe\u003c-function(dfr,delim=\"\\t\"){return(readr::format_delim(dfr,delim=delim));}\n\nwriteToStr_Matrix\u003c-function(m,delim=\"\\t\"){\n  str = \"\";\n  for (r in 1:nrow(m)){\n    str = paste(str,paste(m[r,],collapse=\"\\t\"),sep=\"\\n\")\n  }\n  return(str)\n}\nwriteToStr_Matrix(lst$mT)\ncat(writeToStr_Matrix(lst$mT))\n\n"},"elapsedMs":6}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="call_wNc7cRuctf0KtpOyQMRIggLW"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 7.7KB, 112 lines, showing lines 1-112]\n1\t#--state transition matrix examples\n2\tdirP...[truncated, total 8227 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 7.7KB, 112 lines, showing lines 1-112]\n#--state transition matrix examples\ndirPrj = here::here();\nsource(file.path(dirPrj,\"R/Functions-Text.R\"));\nsource(file.path(dirPrj,\"R/Functions-Matrices.R\"));\n\n# example code\n##--define model dimensions\nsex_states = c(\"m\",\"f\");      #--sexes\nmat_states = c(\"imm\",\"mat\");  #--maturity state\npma_states = c(\"ns\",\"os\");    #--\"post molt age\" states (otherwise known as shell condition)\nsize_bins  = seq(27.5,52.5,5) #--size bins\n##--define model frame\ndfrMF = tidyr::expand_grid(x=sex_states,m=mat_states,p=pma_states,z=size_bins) |> \n        dplyr::mutate(id=dplyr::row_number());\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrMF))\n##\n##--create state transitions associated with molting for Chionoecetes spp. (mature crab don't molt)\n###--immature, new shell crab may molt (imm,ns->imm,ns) or not (imm,ns->imm,os)\n###--immature, old shell crab may molt (imm,os->imm,ns) or not (imm,os->imm,os)\n###--mature, new shell crab don't change state (can't molt) (mat,ns->mat,ns)\n###--mature, old shell crab don't change state (can't molt) (mat,os->mat,os)\n###--create complete state transitions map dataframe for molt probabilities\ndfrSTF = tidyr::expand_grid(m=mat_states,p=pma_states) |> \n         dplyr::mutate(sid=dplyr::row_number());\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTF))\n##--create defined state transitions map dataframe for Chionoecetes spp. \n###--immature crab may molt, mature crab don't\ndfrSTs = tibble::tribble(~from,~to,~val,\n                            1,   1,  1,   # T(1,1) = p_molt(ns)              \u003c= imm,ns can molt, stays imm,ns\n                            1,   2, -1,   # T(1,2) = 1-Sum_{j ne 2}{T[2,j]}  \u003c= imm,ns doesn't molt, becomes imm,os\n                         #  1,   3,  0,   # T(1,3) = 0                       \u003c= imm,ns can't become mat,ns\n                         #  1,   4,  0,   # T(1,4) = 0                       \u003c= imm,ns can't become mat,os\n                            2,   1,  1,   # T(2,1) = p_molt(os)              \u003c= imm,os can molt, becomes imm,ns\n                            2,   2, -1);  # T(2,2) = 1-Sum_{j ne 2}{T[2,j]}  \u003c= imm,os doesn't molt, stays imm,os\n                         #  2,   3,  0,   # T(2,3) = 0                       \u003c= imm,os can't become mat,ns\n                         #  2,   4,  0,   # T(2,4) = 0                       \u003c= imm,os can't become mat,os\n                         #  3,   1,  0,   # T(3,1) = 0                       \u003c= mat,ns can't become imm,ns\n                         #  3,   2,  0,   # T(3,2) = 0                       \u003c= mat,ns can't become imm,os\n                         #  3,   3,  0,   # T(3,3) = 1                       \u003c= mat,ns stays mat,ns\n                         #  3,   4,  0,   # T(3,4) = 0                       \u003c= mat,ns can't become mat,os\n                         #  4,   1,  0,   # T(4,1) = 0                       \u003c= mat,os can't become imm,ns\n                         #  4,   2,  0,   # T(4,2) = 0                       \u003c= mat,os can't become imm,os\n                         #  4,   3,  0,   # T(4,3) = 0                       \u003c= mat,os can't become mat,ns\n                         #  4,   4,  0,   # T(4,4) = 1                       \u003c= mat,os stays mat,os\n cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTs))\n lst = createStateTransitionMatrixMap(dfrSTF,dfrSTs);\n cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(lst$dfrT))\n cat(writeToStr_Matrix(lst$mT))\n##\n##--create state transitions associated with maturation for Chionoecetes spp.\n###--immature, new shell crab may mature (imm,ns->mat,ns) or stay immature (imm,ns->imm,ns)\n###--immature, old shell crab can not mature (didn't molt) (imm,os->imm,os)\n###--mature, new shell crab become old shell (didn't molt) (mat,ns->mat,os)\n###--mature, old shell crab stay old shell (didn't molt) (mat,os->mat,os)\n###--create complete state transitions map dataframe for maturity probabilities\ndfrSTF = tidyr::expand_grid(m=mat_states,p=pma_states) |> \n         dplyr::mutate(sid=dplyr::row_number());\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTF))\n##--create defined state transitions map dataframe\ndfrSTs = tibble::tribble(~from,~to,~val,\n                            1,   1, -1,   # T(1,1) = 1-Sum_{j ne 1}{T[1,j]} \u003c= imm,ns stays imm,ns w/ prob 1-p_mature\n                         #  1,   2,  0,   # T(1,2) = 0                      \u003c= imm,ns can't become imm,os\n                            1,   3,  1,   # T(1,3) = p_mature               \u003c= imm,ns becomes mat,ns w/ prob p_mature\n                         #  1,   4,  0,   # T(1,4) = 0                      \u003c= imm,ns can't become mat,os\n                         #  2,   1,  0,   # T(2,1) = 0                      \u003c= imm,os can't become imm,ns\n                         #  2,   2,  1,   # T(2,2) = 1                      \u003c= imm,os stays imm,os\n                         #  2,   3,  0,   # T(2,3) = 0                      \u003c= imm,os can't become mat,ns\n                         #  2,   4,  0,   # T(2,4) = 0                      \u003c= imm,os can't become mat,os\n                         #  3,   1,  0,   # T(3,1) = 0                      \u003c= mat,ns can't become imm,ns\n                         #  3,   2,  0,   # T(3,2) = 0                      \u003c= mat,ns can't become imm,os\n                         #  3,   3,  0,   # T(3,3) = 0                      \u003c= mat,ns can't stay mat,ns\n                            3,   4,  1);  # T(3,4) = 1                      \u003c= mat,ns must become mat,os\n                         #  4,   1,  0,   # T(4,1) = 0                      \u003c= mat,os can't become imm,ns\n                         #  4,   2,  0,   # T(4,2) = 0                      \u003c= mat,os can't become imm,os\n                         #  4,   3,  0,   # T(4,3) = 0                      \u003c= mat,os can't become mat,ns\n                         #  4,   4,  1,   # T(4,4) = 1                      \u003c= mat,os stays mat,os\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTs))\nlst = createStateTransitionMatrixMap(dfrSTF,dfrSTs);\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(lst$dfrT))\ncat(writeToStr_Matrix(lst$mT))\n##\n## Add a third shell condition and create a molt \npma_states = c(\"ns\",\"os1\",\"os2\");    #--\"post molt age\" states\n##--create (new) model frame\ndfrMF = tidyr::expand_grid(x=sex_states,m=mat_states,p=pma_states,z=size_bins) |> \n        dplyr::mutate(id=dplyr::row_number());\n##--create state transitions associated with molting based Chionoecetes spp. (mature crab don't molt)\n###--imm, ns crab  may molt (imm,ns ->imm,ns) or not (imm,ns ->imm,os1)\n###--imm, os1 crab may molt (imm,os1->imm,ns) or not (imm,os1->imm,os2)\n###--imm, os2 crab may molt (imm,os2->imm,ns) or not (imm,os2->imm,os2)\n###--mature crab don't molt (can't change state) (mat,.->mat,.)\n###--create complete state transitions map dataframe for molt probabilities\ndfrSTF = tidyr::expand_grid(m=mat_states,p=pma_states) |> \n         dplyr::mutate(sid=dplyr::row_number());\ncat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTF))\n##--create defined state transitions map dataframe for Chionoecetes spp. \n###--immature crab may molt, mature crab don't\ndfrSTs = tibble::tribble(~from,~to,~val,\n                            1,   1,  1,   # T(1,1) = p_molt(ns)              \u003c= imm,ns can molt, stays imm,ns\n                            1,   2, -1,   # T(1,2) = 1-Sum_{j ne 2}{T[1,j]}  \u003c= imm,ns doesn't molt, becomes imm,os1\n                            2,   1,  1,   # T(2,1) = p_molt(os)              \u003c= imm,os1 can molt, becomes imm,ns\n                            2,   3, -1,   # T(2,3) = 1-Sum_{j ne 3}{T[2,j]}  \u003c= imm,os1 doesn't molt, becomes imm,os2\n                            3,   1,  1,   # T(3,1) = p_molt(os)              \u003c= imm,os2 can molt, becomes imm,ns\n                            3,   3, -1);  # T(3,3) = 1-Sum_{j ne 3}{T[3,j]}  \u003c= imm,os2 doesn't molt, stays imm,os2\n cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrSTs))\n lst = createStateTransitionMatrixMap(dfrSTF,dfrSTs);\n cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(lst$dfrT))\n cat(writeToStr_Matrix(lst$mT))\n dfrExpMF = dplyr::left_join(dfrMF,lst$dfrT,by=c(\"m\",\"p\"));\n cat(writeToStr_Dataframe(dfrExpMF)) \n \n"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>
